[5003] TOOLRESULT: 
The single-relation setter `set<Relation>(object, relatedObject)` calls `relatedObject.identifier()` without a null check, so passing `null` throws a `NullPointerException`. This is by design: set needs a value. Clear an optional single relation with `unset<Relation>(object)` (generated when unsetting is allowed). `add`/`remove` on multi-valued relations also call `identifier()` per element, so a `null` element fails.

async function openAdminOrder(page: Page, number: string) {
  await page.getByRole('button', { name: 'All orders', exact: true }).first().click();
  await expect(rows(page).first()).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  const cell = page.getByRole('gridcell', { name: number, exact: true });
  for (let i = 0; i < 2 && !(await cell.isVisible()); i++) {
    await page.getByRole('columnheader', { name: /Ordered/ }).click();
    await page.waitForTimeout(1200);
  }
  await apiHelper.triggerAndWaitWithRegex(page, cell, /.*~get$/);
  await expect(page.getByRole('textbox', { name: /^Number/ })).toHaveValue(number);
}
}

[5090] TOOLRESULT: 
- The attribute is a stored (not derived) member of the entity, required, usually an `Integer` or `Long`. It may be marked as the entity's identifier attribute.
- Its default expression reads the `SEQUENCE` variable category: `<Type>!getVariable("SEQUENCE", "<SequenceName>")`. The platform documents `SEQUENCE` as one of the variable categories (`SYSTEM`, `ENVIRONMENT`, `REQUEST`, `ACTOR`, `PRINCIPAL`, `SEQUENCE`), backed by database sequences. `getVariable` is typed by the primitive it is called on and returns a scalar; an undefined variable resolves to `null` with a warning ([model development guide](../../agent-docs/model/model-development.md)).
- Use one sequence name per number space, typically the entity name. Two entities that share a name share a counter.

[5106] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/model/ui-authoring-guide.md", "section": "UI Authoring Guide > TL;DR \u2014 Invariants every authoring step must respect"}
[5107] TOOLRESULT: 
1.  **Every mapped `TransferObjectType` that participates in UI scaffolds exactly three siblings:** `<form>`, `<table>`, `<view>`. These three tags are typed by **containment** on `TransferObjectType` and therefore carry **no `xsi:type`**.
2.  **`xsi:type` is required iff the containing EReference is abstract.** See the Concrete-vs-Abstract Containment table below.
3.  **Every UI leaf that shows data must bind to a `structure::` element** via `dataFeature`, `relationFeature`, `operation`, `access`, `target`, `hiddenBy`, `enabledBy`, or `requiredBy`. A UI element that binds nothing is almost certainly a mistake.
4.  **Grid is 12 columns.** Sum of sibling `col` within one horizontal row must be `≤ 12`. `row > 0`.
5.  **`hiddenBy` / `enabledBy` / `requiredBy` / `confirmationCondition` must reference a boolean `DataMember` on the owner `TransferObjectType`.** No cross-TO references.
6.  **Names are unique within a container.** Duplicate `name` on sibling components is a validation error.
7.  **Use the canonical naming convention** so generators and downstream tooling find elements reliably: `<Entity>_Form`, `<Entity>_Table`, `<Entity>_View_Edit`. In particular, **do not leave bare `View` / `Form` / `Table` names on siblings sharing a namespace** — their FQNs (`<package>::View`, `<package>::Table`, `<package>::Form`) collide across TOs and break downstream resolution. Name UI container elements `<TO>View` / `<TO>Form` / `<TO>Table` **before** adding components, because renaming a

[5129] TOOLRESULT: 
JUDO derives the actor from the token's `azp` claim: dashes become dots and the result must equal an actor FQN. A frontend client with another name cannot be resolved. Set the whitelist `JUDO_PLATFORM_ACCEPTABLE_CLIENTS` (OSGi/Guice key `acceptableClients`, default empty):

```
ActorTypeFQN=client1,client2;OtherActor=client3
```

Client ids are normalised (dash to dot) and an ambiguous client-to-actor mapping is rejected. The Keycloak login interceptor also accepts a `clientToActorMap` that renames `azp` values to actor names. See [OSGi Runtime Services](../deployment/osgi-runtime-services.md#keycloak-connector-and-realm-synchronizer-configuration) for the realm side.

## Intent

Business rules depend on "now": the next working day, a time window, an expiry. If each class reads `LocalDate.now()` itself, tests and demos cannot move time and different classes can disagree on the time zone. Keep one clock in one place, read it everywhere, and allow a start-up override so a test or demo can run at a chosen date.

## Practice

### Platform base

- `Timestamp!now()` in model expressions follows the platform parameter `defaultZon
[5140] TOOLRESULT: ## Overview

Scheduled jobs are OSGi Declarative Services components in a dedicated scheduler module. Each job implements `Runnable`, is configured through OSGi Configuration Admin, and calls one shared business service. The job itself holds no business logic.

## Implementation Pattern

**Job component:**
- Expose the job as a `Runnable` service. Set the component to require its configuration, so the job starts only when its cron expression is provided.
- Turn off overlapping runs of the same job with the scheduler's concurrency property.
- Use a typed configuration definition for each tunable value (cron, thresholds, batch size).

```java
@Component(immediate = true, configurationPid = "example.job.cleanup",
           configurationPolicy = ConfigurationPolicy.REQUIRE,
           property = { "scheduler.concurrent:Boolean=false" },
           service = Runnable.class)
@Designate(ocd = CleanupJobConfig.class)
public class StaleRecordCleanupJob implements Runnable {

    @Reference
    private StaleRecordService staleRecordService; // example project code

    @Override
    public void run() {
        try {
            staleRecordService.purgeStaleRecords();
        } catch (Exception e) {
            LOG.error("Cleanup job failed", e); // never let the scheduler see the exception
        }
    }
}
```

`StaleRecordService` and the class names are example project code, not platform API.

**Business logic:**
- The service holds the work and reads the DAOs of the generated SDK

[5187] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md", "section": "Backend Platform Behavior Reference > Optimistic Locking and `__version`"}
[5187] CALL kb_get: {"path": "model-blueprints/two-call-generation-state/backend.md", "section": "Backend guidance > Atomic state change"}
[5188] TOOLRESULT: 
- Optimistic locking is **on by default** in the RDBMS runtime (`rdbmsDaoOptimisticLockEnabled = true`). Inserts set the version to 1; each write increments it.
- The check happens in `UpdatePayloadDaoProcessor` and **only when the update payload contains `__version`**: it must equal the stored version, otherwise `IllegalArgumentException("Outdated instance to update")`. If `__version` is absent, no check is performed, so omitting it silently disables the guard.
- At SQL level the `VERSION` predicate is added only to the meta `UPDATE` (`WHERE ID = :id AND VERSION = :__version`), not to the attribute `UPDATE`. A stale version makes the statement update no rows, and the executor fails with "There is illegal state, no records updated".
- Clients must echo `__version` back and handle the stale-update error. Custom update operations that rebuild payloads must carry the current version.
[5189] TOOLRESULT: 
Mark the Handle CONFIRMED in the same transaction as the Document creation, or before it. The check "state is ACTIVE" and the update must not be separable, otherwise two concurrent confirms can both pass. Platform optimistic locking helps here: the update fails when the Handle changed in between, provided the version is carried in the update payload, see [platform behavior reference](../../agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md) (optimistic locking). If the update omits the version the guard is off.

public class ExpirePointsCustomImplementation implements com.example.webshop.webshop.operation.webshop.dashboardinfo.ExpirePoints {
    public shopmessage.ShopMessage apply(dashboardinfo.DashboardInfo _this) throws shopfault.ShopFaultException {
    @Reference AdminAccountInfoDao adminAccountInfoDao;
    @Reference AdminAccountDao adminAccountDao;

    @Override
    public ShopMessage apply(InviteAdminInput input) throws ShopFaultException {
        String email = Emails.normalize(input.getEmail());
        if (!Emails.looksValid(email)) {
            throw new ShopFaultException("INVALID_EMAIL", ShopFault.builder().withMessage("Please enter a valid e-mail address.").build());
        }
        if (adminAccountDao.query().filterByEmail(StringFilter.equalTo(email)).count() > 0) {
            return ShopMessage.builder().withMessage(email + " is already an admin.").build();
        }
        String name = java.util.Optional.ofNullable(input.getName()).flatMap(n -> n).map(String::trim).filter(n -> !n.isEmpty()).orElse(email);
        adminAccountInfoDao.create(AdminAccountInfoForCreate.builder().withEmail(email).withName(name).build());
        return ShopMessage.builder().withMessage("Invited " + email + ".").build();
    }

[5318] TOOLRESULT: export const NotificationBell: FC = () => {
  const { t } = useTranslation();
  const { clearNavigate } = useJudoNavigation();
  const location = useLocation();
  const [count, setCount] = useState(0);
  const service = useMemo(() => new CustomerServiceForProfileImpl(judoAxiosProvider), []);

  const load = useCallback(() => {
    service
      .refreshForProfile({ _mask: '{unreadNotifications}' } as any)
      .then((r) => setCount(r.data.unreadNotifications ?? 0))
      .catch(() => undefined); // the badge is a hint: a failed load must not disturb the page
  }, [service]);

  useEffect(() => {
    load();
  }, [load, location.pathname, location.key]);
  useEffect(() => {
    const timer = window.setInterval(load, 15000);
    return () => window.clearInterval(timer);
  }, [load]);

  const label = t('shop.notifications.unread', { defaultValue: 'Unread notifications' }) as string;
  return (
    <IconButton
      color="inherit"
      aria-label={`${label}: ${count}`}
      data-testid="notification-bell"
      onClick={() => clearNavigate(routeToCustomerNotificationsAccessTablePage())}
    >
      <Badge badgeContent={count} color="secondary" invisible={count === 0} data-testid="unread-badge">
        <NotificationsIcon />
      </Badge>
    </IconButton>
  );
};

# Args:
# 1 - instance name
dump_postgresql () {
    local INSTANCE_NAME=$1
    local TIMESTAMP=$( date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S )

    echo "Dumping database..."
    local DUMP_FILE=${schema_name}_dump_$TIMESTAMP.tar.gz
    docker exec -i ${INSTANCE_NAME} /bin/bash -c "PGPASSWORD=${schema_name} pg_dump --username=${schema_name} -F c ${schema_name}" > $DUMP_FILE || exit
    echo "Database dumped to $DUMP_FILE"
}

upgrade_postgresql_schema () {
    mvnd judo-rdbms-schema:apply \
    ${mavenVersionArg} \
    -DjdbcUrl=jdbc:postgresql://127.0.0.1:${POSTGRES_PORT:-5432}/${schema_name} \
    -DdbType=postgresql \
    -DdbUser=${schema_name} \
    -DdbPassword=${schema_name} \
    -DschemaIgnoreModelDependency=true \
    -DupdateModel=${SCHEMA_DIR}/model/target/generated-resources/model/${schema_name}-rdbms_postgresql.model \
    -f ${SCHEMA_DIR}/schema || exit
}

install_maven_wrapper () {
    mvn wrapper:wrapper -Dmaven=3.8.6 || exit
    sed $(get_sed_edit_option) 's/https:\/\/nexus\.judo\.technology\/repository\/maven-judong\//https:\/\/repo\.maven\.apache\.org\/maven2\//; s/https\:\/\//####/; s/\/\//\//; s/####/https\:\/\//' ${MODEL_DIR}/.mvn/wrapper/maven-wrapper.properties
}

[5353] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/integration-testing/getting-started.md", "section": "Integration Testing - Getting Started Guide > Testing Fundamentals > Transaction Handling Modes > 3. `MANUAL`"}
[5354] TOOLRESULT: 
```java
@JudoTest(transaction = TransactionHandling.MANUAL)
public class MyTest {
    @Test
    public void testWithManualControl(JudoRuntimeFixture fixture) {
        fixture.beginTransaction();
        try {
            // Your test logic
            fixture.commitTransaction();
        } catch (Exception e) {
            fixture.rollbackTransaction();
            throw e;
        }
    }
}
```

**Behavior**:
- No automatic transaction management
- You control `beginTransaction()`, `commitTransaction()`, and `rollbackTransaction()` on the `JudoRuntimeFixture`

**Use when**:
- Testing complex multi-transaction scenarios
- Verifying transaction boundary behavior
- Testing error recovery

// The shop front: actor Customer (F14). Users: customer1/customer1 and customer2/customer2
// (scripts/seed-dev-user.sh); anybody can sign up (F13). The password equals the user name.
export async function login(page: Page, user = 'customer1') {
  await apiHelper.gotoWithWait(page); // app start URL, redirects to Keycloak
  await expect(page.locator('#kc-form-login')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  await page.locator('#username').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#password').fill(user);
  await page.locator('#kc-login').click();
  await expect(page.getByTestId('catalog-grid').or(page.getByTestId('catalog-empty'))).toBeVisible({ timeout: 30000 }); // the shop home is the card grid
}

// The generated drawer animates its width after a resize; take the screenshot only when the width stopped changing.
export async function settleLayout(page: Page) {
  const width = page.viewportSize()?.width ?? 0;
  if (width >= 1200) {
    // laptop and wide screens: the drawer is open once the logo has its full width
    await expect
      .poll(async () => (await page.locator('img[src*="trail-shop-logo"]').first().boundingBox().catch(() => null))?.width ?? 0, { timeout: 8000 })
      .toBeGreaterThanOrEqual(120)
      .catch(() => undefined);
  }
  await page.waitForTimeout(500);
}

Restrict what an actor can reach by giving its access a `DERIVED` type and a getter expression, so visibility is enforced in the model.

[5440] CALL read: {"path": "/Users/bmiller/Projects/judo-blueprint/catalog/best-practices/model/derived-access-filtering.md", "offset": 14, "limit": 60}
[5441] TOOLRESULT: diagram: { kinds: [entity, enum, transfer, actor], members: types }
first_seen: 2026-03-04
last_updated: 2026-11-10
---
## Intent

Restrict what an actor can reach by giving its access a `DERIVED` type and a getter expression, so visibility is enforced in the model.

## Practice

- An actor access has `accessType` `ALL` or `DERIVED`. `ALL` is fixed to `0..*` and has no getter; `DERIVED` has a configurable cardinality (`0..1` or `0..*`) and a getter ([accesspoint.md](../../agent-docs/model/esm_metamodel/accesspoint.md), *AccessType Behaviour*).
- Getter rules, all at once ([architectural patterns](../../agent-docs/backend/architectural-patterns.md), *Rules for `accesses[*].getterExpression`*):
  - the filter starts from the entity, not from the transfer object
  - `self` is not in scope inside an actor access getter; iterate from the entity with a lambda variable
  - a `DERIVED` access needs an expression that returns references; for "see everything" use `ALL` with no getter
  - `!getVariable(...)` returns a scalar; compare with `==`, and qualify the primitive type
- Typical shapes:
  - status filter: `Contest!filter(c | c.status == ContestStatus#OPEN)`
  - singleton: an expression ending in `!any()` with cardinality `0..1`
  - ownership: compare an entity field to the actor's own value, for example `!getVariable("ACTOR", "email")`
  - role-scoped: one `!filter` whose lambda disjuncts a role flag and the ownership clause; JQL has no if/else ([advanced modeling patterns](../../
[5442] CALL kb_get: {"path": "agent-docs/backend/platform-behavior-reference.md", "section": "Backend Platform Behavior Reference > Signed Identifiers"}
[5443] TOOLRESULT: 
- Every instance the REST layer returns carries `__identifier` and a `__signedIdentifier`. Bound operations and instance updates take the signed identifier (request header `X-Judo-SignedIdentifier`), not the plain id.
- The signature covers the id, the entity type, the entity **`__version`** and an **immutable** flag (`DefaultIdentifierSigner`). Because the version is part of the token, **the signed identifier changes after every update** of the entity. A signed identifier you stored earlier can therefore differ from the current one.
- Signing uses a key: a configured HMAC `secret` or, if none is configured, a key generated at start. With a generated key all previously issued signed identifiers become invalid after a restart, and nodes of a cluster cannot validate each other's tokens. Configure a stable secret in production.
- Server or client code **cannot mint** a signed identifier; the runtime signs identifiers only while serving a response. Code that needs the signed id of an instance must obtain it from a response of the API.
- `immutable`: instances produced from an `immutable` copy carry the flag inside the signed identifier. The dispatcher refuses exposed operations on such instances with `AccessDeniedException` (`BOUND_OPERATION_INSTANCE_IS_IMMUTABLE`).
- A signed instance of a bound operation must come from a transfer object exposed to the calling actor, else `ACCESS_DENIED_FOR_INSTANCE_OF_BOUND_OPERATION` (403).

[5451] TOOLRESULT: ### The Interceptor Contract

| Method | Meaning |
| :--- | :--- |
| `getName()` | Name of the interceptor (used for logging). |
| `getOperations(AsmModel)` | Operations the interceptor is registered for. The default is an empty collection, which means **all intercepted (built-in) operations**. |
| `preCall` | Runs before the decorated call. The returned object replaces the input payload and must be assignable to the expected payload type (otherwise `IllegalArgumentException`). When `ignoreDecoratedCall()` is `true` it must return the payload of the decorated call's return type. |
| `postCall` | Runs after the decorated call with the input and the call's return payload (a `Payload` or a collection of payloads). The returned object becomes the result. |
| `ignoreDecoratedCall()` | `true` skips the platform's own DAO call, so the interceptor replaces the operation. The decorated call runs only if no interceptor for the operation returns `true`. |
| `async()` | `true` runs the interceptor on a different thread, outside the transaction and without context variables. Its exceptions are lost and it cannot change the payload. |
| `terminateOnException()` | Defaults to `true`: an exception terminates the call. Ignored for async interceptors. |

Interceptors may throw only `InterceptorCallBusinessException` (see [Throwing a Business Error from an Interceptor](#throwing-a-business-error-from-an-interceptor)).

### Which Operations Are Intercepted

[5452] TOOLRESULT: ## Throwing a Business Error from an Interceptor

`preCall` and `postCall` declare `throws InterceptorCallBusinessException` (package `hu.blackbelt.judo.runtime.core.dispatcher.behaviours`, a subclass of the platform `BusinessException`). It is the **only** business-level exception an interceptor may throw:

- A modeled fault's generated `<Fault>Exception` is checked and cannot be thrown from an interceptor; catch it from the service you called and rewrap it.
- A raw `RuntimeException` (or `IllegalStateException`) is wrapped by the dispatcher as `InternalServerException` and the client sees a generic 500.
- Interceptors do not handle business problems on their own; they refuse the call by wrapping the problem.

```java
throw new InterceptorCallBusinessException(
        "<app>.services.AlreadyShipped",                 // type: FQN of a modeled fault transfer object
        "ALREADY_SHIPPED",                               // errorCode
        Map.of("message", "The order was already shipped"), // details; never null
        null,                                            // cause (optional)
        null);                                           // locale: replaced by the dispatcher's locale
```

What happens next: the behaviour call catches the exception and returns a `_fault` payload; `DefaultDispatcher` turns it into a `BusinessException`; REST answers **422** with header `X-Fault: <type>` and body `{ "<type>": {details without "_"-prefixed keys and nulls}, "code": "<erro

async function clearRates(a: Admin) {
  await menu(a.page, 'Exchange rates').click();
  await expect(a.page.getByText(/Rows per page/)).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
  for (let guard = 0; guard < 20; guard++) {
    const del = a.page.getByRole('row').filter({ has: a.page.getByRole('gridcell') }).first().getByRole('button', { name: /Delete/ });
    if ((await del.count()) === 0) break;
    await del.click();
    const confirm = a.page.getByRole('dialog').getByRole('button', { name: /(Ok|Delete|Yes|Confirm)$/ });
    if (await confirm.isVisible().catch(() => false)) await confirm.click();
    await a.page.waitForTimeout(1200);
  }
  await expect(a.page.getByText('No results found.')).toBeVisible({ timeout: 15000 });
}
